class Solution:
    def getAncestors(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        #Use Kahn's algorithm of toposort using a queue and bfs!
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        indegrees = [0] * n
        
        #1st step: build adjacency list grpah and update the initial indegrees of every node!
        for edge in edges:
            src, dest = edge[0], edge[1]
            graph[src].append(dest)
            indegrees[dest] += 1
        
        
        queue = deque()
        ans = [set() for _ in range(n)]
        #2nd step: go through the indegrees array and add to queue for any node that has no ancestor!
        for i in range(len(indegrees)):
            if(indegrees[i] == 0):
                queue.append(i)
        
        #Kahn's algorithm initiation!
        while queue:
            cur = queue.pop()
            
            #for each neighbor
            for neighbor in graph[cur]:
                #current node is ancestor to each and every neighboring node!
                ans[neighbor].add(cur)
                #every ancestor of current node is also an ancestor to the neighboring node!
                ans[neighbor].update(ans[cur])
                indegrees[neighbor] -= 1
                if(indegrees[neighbor] == 0):
                    queue.append(neighbor)
        
        #at the end, we should have set of ancestors for each and every node!
        #in worst case, set s for ith node could have all other vertices be ancestor to node i !
        ans = [(sorted(list(s))) for s in myList]
        return ans

    def getAncestors(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        #Use Kahn's algorithm of toposort using a queue and bfs!
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n)]

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def getAncestors(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        indegrees = [0] * n

        for src, dest in edges:
            graph[src].append(dest)
            indegrees[dest] += 1

        q = deque()
        ans = [set() for _ in range(n)]

        for i in range(n):
            if indegrees[i] == 0:
                q.append(i)

        while q:
            cur = q.popleft()

            for neighbor in graph[cur]:
                ans[neighbor].add(cur)
                ans[neighbor].update(ans[cur])

                indegrees[neighbor] -= 1
                if indegrees[neighbor] == 0:
                    q.append(neighbor)

        return [sorted(s) for s in ans]
```

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def getAncestors(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        indegrees = [0] * n

        for src, dest in edges:
            graph[src].append(dest)
            indegrees[dest] += 1

        q = deque()
        ans = [set() for _ in range(n)]

        for i in range(n):
            if indegrees[i] == 0:
                q.append(i)

        while q:
            cur = q.popleft()

            for neighbor in graph[cur]:
                ans[neighbor].add(cur)
                ans[neighbor].update(ans[cur])

                indegrees[neighbor] -= 1
                if indegrees[neighbor] == 0:
                    q.append(neighbor)

        return [sorted(s) for s in ans]

Let's think if there is a more subtle bug: In Kahn's algorithm, when processing `cur`, we update `ans[neighbor]` with `ans[cur]`. But what if `cur` has ancestors that are also ancestors of `neighbor` via another path, and those ancestors are not yet in `ans[cur]` because `cur` was enqueued before one of its ancestors was processed? We argued impossible because indegree zero means all direct predecessors processed. But let's rigorously prove with stack/popleft:
- Each node has indegree count equal to number of incoming edges from all direct predecessors.
- When a direct predecessor p is processed, it decrements indegree of v for each edge p->v.
- v is enqueued only when indegree[v] becomes 0, which means every incoming edge has been processed, i.e., every direct predecessor p has been processed.
- When p was processed, it added all its ancestors to ans[p] (by induction). Thus ans[v] has all direct predecessors and all their ancestors. That includes all ancestors of v (any ancestor of v must be ancestor of some direct predecessor? If a is ancestor of v, path a -> ... -> v. Let p be the last node before v on path, then p is direct predecessor of v and a is ancestor of p. So yes). Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
should be code only. Need not include reasoning. Need ensure no weird "are response" from my thought. Final code.

Let's produce final code.</think>```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def getAncestors(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        indegrees = [0] * n

        for src, dest in edges:
            graph[src].append(dest)
            indegrees[dest] += 1

        q = deque()
        ans = [set() for _ in range(n)]

        for i in range(n):
            if indegrees[i] == 0:
                q.append(i)

        while q:
            cur = q.popleft()

            for neighbor in graph[cur]:
                ans[neighbor].add(cur)
                ans[neighbor].update(ans[cur])

                indegrees[neighbor] -= 1
                if indegrees[neighbor] == 0:
                    q.append(neighbor)

        return [sorted(s) for s in ans]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def getAncestors(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        indegrees = [0] * n

        for src, dest in edges:
            graph[src].append(dest)
            indegrees[dest] += 1

        q = deque()
        ans = [set() for _ in range(n)]

        for i in range(n):
            if indegrees[i] == 0:
                q.append(i)

        while q:
            cur = q.popleft()

            for neighbor in graph[cur]:
                ans[neighbor].add(cur)
                ans[neighbor].update(ans[cur])

                indegrees[neighbor] -= 1
                if indegrees[neighbor] == 0:
                    q.append(neighbor)

        return [sorted(s) for s in ans]
